# Function to read with timeout and default
read_with_timeout() {
    local prompt="$1"
    local default="$2"
    local timeout="$3"
    local reply

    # Read with timeout in background
    read -t "$timeout" -p "$prompt" reply &

    # Show countdown
    for ((i = timeout; i > 0; i--)); do
        printf "\r%s (auto-%s in %ds) " "$prompt" "$default" "$i"
        sleep 1
    done
    printf "\r%s (auto-%s)     \n" "$prompt" "$default"

    # Get the reply or use default
    if [ -z "$reply" ]; then
        reply="$default"
    fi

    echo "$reply"
}

# Get real user (not root, not sudo) (from common_functions.sh)
get_real_user_from_common_functions() {
    local real_user=""
    local real_home=""

    # Priority 1: Check SUDO_USER environment variable
    if [ -n "${SUDO_USER:-}" ] && [ "$SUDO_USER" != "root" ]; then
        real_user="$SUDO_USER"
        real_home="$(getent passwd "$real_user" 2>/dev/null | cut -d: -f6)"
        if [ -n "$real_home" ] && [ -d "$real_home" ]; then
            echo "$real_user"
            return 0
        fi
    fi

    # Priority 2: If not root, return current user
    if [ "$(id -u)" -ne 0 ] && [ "$USER" != "root" ]; then
        echo "$USER"
        return 0
    fi

            local username="$(basename "$user_home")"

    if [ "$IS_WSL" = true ]; then
        # In WSL, check if X11 forwarding is available or if WSLg is running
        if [ -n "${DISPLAY:-}" ] || [ -n "${WAYLAND_DISPLAY:-}" ] || pgrep -x "wslg" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
            HAS_DESKTOP_ENVIRONMENT=true
        fi
    fi
}

I found relevant information about GNOME power and sleep settings. Here are the key findings:

## GNOME gsettings Commands for Power Management

To disable automatic suspend when the system is running on AC power, use: `gsettings set org.gnome.settings-daemon.plugins.power sleep-inactive-ac-type 'nothing'`

To set a screen blank timeout to 600 seconds (10 minutes), use: `gsettings set org.gnome.desktop.session idle-delay 600`

## Related Settings

You can query AC and battery timeout settings with `gsettings get org.gnome.settings-daemon.plugins.power sleep-inactive-ac-timeout` and `gsettings get org.gnome.settings-daemon.plugins.power sleep-inactive-battery-timeout`. The AC timeout applies to AC power settings, and the battery timeout applies to battery power.

## System-Wide Configuration

For system-wide settings on the login screen (GDM), to disable auto-suspending for the greeter screen, run as root: `su gdm -s /bin/bash -c "dbus-run-session gsettings set org.gnome.settings-daemon.plugins.power sleep-inactive-ac-type nothing"`

These settings can also be managed through the dconf Editor GUI tool for GNOME desktop environments.

Based on the web search results, here's information about using systemd to disable sleep, suspend, and hibernate modes along with logind.conf configuration:

## Masking Sleep Targets with systemctl

You can mask (or block) the sleep, suspend, hibernate, and hybrid-sleep features, and the mask operation creates a symbolic link that points to /dev/null for the specified target or service. The common command used is:

```bash
sudo systemctl mask sleep.target suspend.target hibernate.target hybrid-sleep.target
```

To re-enable these features, you can remove the symbolic link that points to /dev/null through the unmasking technique using: `sudo systemctl unmask sleep.target suspend.target hibernate.target hybrid-sleep.target`

239	disable_disk_spindown() {
240	    if ! command -v hdparm >/dev/null 2>&1; then
241	        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] hdparm not installed; skipping disk spindown control."

245	    local persisted_any=false

249	        [ -e "$disk" ] || continue
250	        dev="/dev/$(basename "$disk")"
251	        rota="$(cat "$disk/queue/rotational" 2>/dev/null || echo 1)"
252	        rm="$(cat "$disk/removable" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)"

255	            continue
256	        fi

Done. Here's what changed in `scripts/shells/linux/debian/install_shells/` (the orchestrator in `install.sh`/`install_test_menu.sh` discovers scripts dynamically by numeric prefix, so renumbering is all that's needed — no manifest to edit).

## 1. Merged the three cloud-monitor stops → `3_stop_cloud_monitors.sh`
- Combined `3_stop_aliyun_cloudmonitor.sh` + `4_stop_huawei_cloudmonitor.sh` + `5_stop_tencent_cloudmonitor.sh` into one renamed script with three functions (Aliyun/Aegis, Huawei/Hostguard, Tencent/Sgagent·Barad·YunJing·TAT).
- **Idempotent / skip-if-OK**: each section starts with a presence check and returns early if nothing is installed; all removals are guarded.
- Dropped script 4's hard `id -u … exit 1` (it would abort the whole install run) in favor of the project's `$USE_SUDO` convention; Aliyun downloads now run in a temp dir instead of CWD.
- Deleted the old 3/4/5.

## 2. New `4_set_desktop_power.sh` (desktop power: never sleep)
Runs only if a graphical desktop is detected (`HAS_DESKTOP_ENVIRONMENT` OR on-disk evidence — `gnome-shell`, `/usr/share/{x,wayland}sessions`, a display-manager unit), else skips cleanly on servers. Settings grounded in official/canonical docs:
- **No suspend/hibernate**: `systemctl mask sleep.target suspend.target hibernate.target hybrid-sleep.target` + a `logind.conf.d` drop-in (`IdleAction=ignore`, `HandleLidSwitch=ignore`, …).
- **No display blank / no screensaver**: system-wide GNOME defaults via the dconf `local` system db (`idle-delay=0`, `sleep-inactive-*-type='nothing'`, screensaver lock off) + best-effort live `gsettings` for the active user.
- **No disk spindown**: best-effort `hdparm -S 0 -B 254` on fixed (non-removable) disks, persisted to `/etc/hdparm.conf`.
- Idempotent throughout (compares current state, re-asserts only on drift).

## 3. Merged mirrors + journal + auto-updates → `6_system_maintenance.sh`
- Combined `6_set_mirrors.sh` + `7_auto_clean_journal.sh` + `9_disable_ubuntu_auto_updates.sh`.
- Made it **fully non-interactive** (removed the old `read -t 10` prompt) and idempotent; honors the APT-repo policy (never deletes distro-shipped sources).
- Deleted the old 7 and 9; repointed the menu reference `DISABLE_UBUNTU_AUTO_UPDATES_SCRIPT_RELATIVE` in `dd_helper/constants.sh` to the merged script.

**Resulting run order:** `1 → 2 → 3_stop_cloud_monitors → 4_set_desktop_power → 6_system_maintenance → 8_project_validator → 10 → 11 …`

All three new scripts pass `bash -n`. The only lingering references to the old names are in auto-generated `*_tree.md` snapshots, which I left untouched per the repo's "never edit *.md" rule (they regenerate from tooling).

One judgment call to flag: the **"disable auto-updates" menu item** now runs the full `6_system_maintenance.sh` (mirrors + journal + updates). It still disables auto-updates and everything is idempotent, but it does slightly more than its label. Say the word if you'd rather that menu action stay narrowly scoped and I'll split it back out.

Done. I reverted the separate `claudeultra` approach and instead made `claudeteam` itself default-enable **multiple roles + ultracode**, on both Linux and Windows.

## What changed

**`scripts/linuxenvs/claudeteam.sh`** (Linux launcher)
- Keeps `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1` (multiple roles).
- Now also passes `--settings '{"ultracode":true}'` by default.
- Rebuilt the invocation as an args array (`claude "${claude_args[@]}" "$@"`) so the JSON is always one argument — the old unquoted-string-split pattern was fragile for JSON.
- Root safety preserved: `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is added only for non-root.

**`scripts/winenvs/claudeteam.ps1`** (Windows launcher)
- Same change: sets the teams env, adds `$ultraSettingsJson = '{"ultracode":true}'`, and invokes `& claude --settings $ultraSettingsJson --dangerously-skip-permissions @args`.
- Updated banner/synopsis to match.

**Reverted** the `claudeultra` additions from the prior step: deleted `linuxenvs/claudeultra.sh` and `winenvs/claudeultra.ps1`, and restored `claude_code_install.sh` (back to linking just `claudeteam`, with an updated comment noting it now launches with multiple roles + ultracode) and the menu label.

## Effective command now
- **non-root / Windows:** `claude --settings '{"ultracode":true}' --dangerously-skip-permissions [args…]`
- **root:** `claude --settings '{"ultracode":true}' [args…]` (skip-permissions dropped, as Claude Code refuses it as root)

## Verification
- `bash -n` passes for all three touched shell scripts.
- Arg simulation confirms the JSON passes as a single token and root vs. user branching is correct.
- `pwsh` parses `claudeteam.ps1` cleanly.
- No leftover `claudeultra` references anywhere.

Wiring is unchanged: `sync_linuxenvs_to_bin` keeps `claudeteam`/`claudeteam.sh` symlinked into `/usr/local/bin` for all users, the "Install Claude Code" menu action still links it, and on Windows `claudeteam.ps1` is exposed via the existing winenvs/`claudelist.ps1` mechanism — so every existing `claudeteam` entry point now gets ultracode + multiple roles automatically.

One note: I left `--teammate-mode in-process` commented out in the Windows script exactly as it already was — say the word if you want that re-enabled alongside the multiple-roles default.

[2026-06-22 15:11:09] ASSISTANT:
This is the key finding: the native `claude` is a **single self-contained ELF binary** (`~/.local/bin/claude` → `~/.local/share/claude/versions/X`). And crucially, `/home/kali` itself is mode `700` — so a *symlink* in `/usr/local/bin` is unreachable by other users even for a **regular-user** install, not just root. The robust fix for true all-users access is to **copy the self-contained binary** into `/usr/local/bin` (works for everyone, no home traversal needed).

Let me read the current step-2 block in `claude_code_install.sh` to replace it:

## Summary

`129_install_claude_code.sh` is now the **all-in-one, idempotent, call-anywhere** Claude Code provisioner, and the dd.sh AI & MCP Management menu calls the same script — so both do all of it:

**1. Native install** — delegates to `claude_code_install.sh` (official `claude.ai/install.sh`), skipped when claude already works.

**2. Sync claude bin to ALL users (regular + root) — with the permission fix** (the core of your request). New `cci_install_claude_all_users` in `claude_code_install.sh`:
- The native build is a **single self-contained ELF binary**, and per-user homes are mode `700` — so a symlink in `/usr/local/bin` is unreachable by other users (true for a root install *and* a regular-user install).
- When the resolved binary isn't world-reachable, it **copies the binary into `/usr/local/bin/claude` (0755)** → works for every user without exposing anyone's home. When it *is* reachable, it symlinks (so native auto-updates track).
- Idempotent: skips the copy when the shared binary is already identical (`cmp -s`); re-run refreshes after an update.

**3. Sync `claudeteam.sh` to all users' bin** — unchanged `cci_setup_claudeteam` links `claudeteam` + `claudeteam.sh` into `/usr/local/bin`.

**4. Install MCP + sync to all AI tools** — 129 now also sources `mcp_sync_engine.sh` and runs `mcp_install_all` (Chrome + Context7 MCP, then sync to all tools), best-effort so MCP issues never fail the claude step.

**Idempotency throughout:** native install skipped if present, shared copy skipped if identical, symlinks only (re)created when missing/wrong, MCP re-asserts config.

**Wiring:** the menu's "Install Claude Code…" entry runs `129_install_claude_code.sh` (callable from the full install pass, dd.sh, or the menu — single source of truth). claude was already removed from 120 in the previous step.

Files changed: `scripts/ai_shtools/claude_code_install.sh` (all-users copy/permission-fix), `scripts/shells/linux/debian/install_shells/129_install_claude_code.sh` (added MCP step + docs). All pass `bash -n`.

One heads-up: since 129 auto-runs in the full install pass and now also installs MCP, that pass will trigger the Chrome MCP build (it runs after node/pnpm at step 15/28, so deps are present; it's guarded `|| true`). If you'd prefer MCP **not** to run during the unattended full install (only when invoked from the menu), I can gate step 4 behind a flag/arg — just say so.

I found relevant search results for your query about pip's "uninstall-no-record-file" error. Here are the key findings:

## The Error

The "uninstall-no-record-file" error occurs when pip cannot uninstall a package because no RECORD file was found. When the package was installed by debian, pip hints that the package was installed by the OS.

## Why This Happens

Pip cannot uninstall a package because it cannot find the installed-files manifest (RECORD) in the package metadata. This usually occurs when the package was installed by a distro package manager that doesn't provide pip uninstall metadata, the package's dist-info metadata was partially removed or corrupted, or you're trying to uninstall a package outside the active environment/prefix
        ... [truncated 676 chars]

[2026-06-22 15:58:54] ASSISTANT:
Now I have the complete picture. The failure is `torch_cpu_guard.sh:108` (the GPU "torch missing" path) — it's the only pip call in that guard **missing `--ignore-installed`**, which is exactly what its sibling functions (lines 81/88) use to avoid uninstalling the distro `mpmath`. And the per-package loop (`ultralytics`, `cnocr`) goes through `run_pip_install_realtime`, which has no repair.

Per the official-docs research, the safe, dpkg-respecting fix is to install the needed version as a pip-managed copy in `/usr/local/.../dist-packages` (which shadows `/usr/lib/.../dist-packages` via sys.path precedence) **without** uninstalling the apt copy — i.e. `--ignore-installed`. Let me apply both fixes.

[2026-06-22 16:00:16] ASSISTANT:
Confirmed: `/usr/local/lib/python3.13/dist-packages` (index 5) precedes the apt `/usr/lib/python3/dist-packages` (index 6), so a pip-installed `mpmath 1.3.0` there shadows the apt `1.4.1` — the fix is sound.

## Root cause
`torch` (and transitively `ultralytics`, `cnocr`) require `mpmath<1.4`, but Debian/Ubuntu/Kali ship `mpmath 1.4.1` in `/usr/lib/python3/dist-packages` **with no RECORD file**. pip tries to uninstall it to install 1.3.0, can't (no manifest), and aborts the whole install with `error: uninstall-no-record-file`. So torch never installed, then ultralytics/cnocr hit the same wall.

## Fixes (idempotent, dpkg-safe, Debian/Ubuntu/Kali)
Grounded in the pip issue tracker / official guidance: don't uninstall the OS-managed package — install the needed version pip-side into `/usr/local/.../dist-packages`, which shadows the apt copy via sys.path precedence, leaving dpkg's files untouched.

**1. `torch_cpu_guard.sh:108`** — the GPU "torch missing" path was the one pip call in that guard missing `--ignore-installed` (its CPU/CUDA siblings already had it). Added `--ignore-installed` so the required `mpmath 1.3.0` lands in `/usr/local` without removing the apt copy. This fixes the logged failure and **cascades**: once `mpmath 1.3.0` is present, the later `ultralytics`/`cnocr` installs see `<1.4` satisfied and never attempt the uninstall.

**2. `run_pip_install_realtime` (general safety net)** — new `pip_repair_no_record_blocker`: on a `no RECORD file` / `uninstall-no-record-file` failure it parses the blocked package **and the exact constraint pip was resolving** (e.g. `mpmath<1.4,>=1.1.0`), installs that surgically with `--ignore-installed --no-deps` into `/usr/local`, then retries the original command once. Surgical (doesn't re-download torch), idempotent, and works for any future distro no-RECORD blocker, not just mpmath.

## Verification
- `bash -n` passes on both files.
- Parser tested against your exact log text → extracts `mpmath` + `<1.4,>=1.1.0` → repair spec `mpmath<1.4,>=1.1.0` (pip fetches 1.3.0).
- Confirmed `/usr/local` shadows `/usr/lib` on this machine.

On the next run, the torch-guard line now succeeds, and even if reached independently, the per-package installer self-repairs and retries.

Note on scope: you mentioned "use workflow / ultracode" — I kept this as a focused, grounded two-file repair rather than spinning up a multi-agent workflow, since the fix is localized to the pip plumbing and benefits more from precise edits + verification than from fan-out. If you'd like, I can run a broader workflow to audit *all* pip-install call sites in the install scripts for the same no-RECORD/PEP-668 hazards and standardize them.

316	# Resolve usable mount path for a device. Prefer CURRENT mount so we use the path where data actually is
317	# (e.g. /media/ubuntu/Soft); std path like /mnt/dev_nvme0n1p4 may exist but be empty until reboot.
318	_resolve_device_mount_path() {
319	    local device="$1"
320	    local std_mount current_mount
321	    std_mount=$(device_to_mount_point "$device")
322	    current_mount=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET "$device" 2>/dev/null || echo "")
323	    if [ -n "$current_mount" ] && [ -d "$current_mount" ] && ( [ -w "$current_mount" ] || [ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ] ); then
324	        echo "$current_mount"
325	        return 0
326	    fi
327	    if [ -d "$std_mount" ] && ( [ -w "$std_mount" ] || [ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ] ); then
328	        echo "$std_mount"
329	        return 0
330	    fi
331	    echo ""
332	    return 1
333	}

[2026-06-22 21:24:18] ASSISTANT:
Confirmed: the only two derivations are the two `device_to_mount_point` functions (the deepseek scripts just glob existing `/mnt/dev_*`, they're consumers). The fix: when a device is **already mounted under the mount base**, that existing path is authoritative — return it instead of re-deriving from the volatile node name. This stops the relocation and self-heals the bad fstab entry. Applying to both copies:

[2026-06-22 21:24:24]   -> tool: Edit  {"replace_all": false, "file_path": "/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node/scripts/shells/linux/debian/install_shells/2_setting_base.sh", "old_string": "device_to_mount_point() {\n    local device=\"$1\"\n    # Convert /dev/sdb3 to dev_sdb3\n    local mount_name=$(echo \"$device\" | sed 's|/dev/|dev_|g')\n    echo \"$DEFAULT_MOUNT_BASE/$mount_name\"\n}", "new_string": "device_to_mount_point() {\n    local device=\"$1\"\n    local mount_base=\"${2:-$DEFAULT_MOUNT_BASE}\"\n    # NVMe/SATA kernel device nodes (/dev/nvme0n1p1 vs /dev/nvme1n1p1, /dev/sdb vs\n    # /dev/sdc) are assigned by PROBE ORDER and are NOT stable across reboots, so a\n    # disk that was nvme0n1p1 yesterday can be nvme1n1p1 today. Deriving the mount\n    # path purely from the node name therefore drifts (nvme0<->nvme1) and makes the\n    # installer try to relocate a live, correctly-mounted disk to a new empty dir.\n    # If the device is ALREADY mounted under the mount base, that existing path is\n    # authoritative (the data lives there and the project depends on it) -> return\n    # it. Only a brand-new / not-yet-mounted disk falls back to the node-derived name.\n    local existing\n    existing=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET \"$device\" 2>/dev/null | head -n1)\n    if [ -n \"$existing\" ] && [ \"${existing#${mount_base}/}\" != \"$existing\" ]; then\n        echo \"$existing\"\n        return 0\n    fi\n    # Convert /dev/sdb3 to dev_sdb3\n    local mount_name=$(echo \"$device\" | sed 's|/dev/|dev_|g')\n    echo \"$mount_base/$mount_name\"\n}"}

[2026-06-22 21:25:20] ASSISTANT:
Both files pass `bash -n`, and the simulation confirms the fix resolves exactly the reported case while preserving behavior for new disks.

## Root cause
The mount path was derived purely from the **kernel device node name** (`/dev/nvmeXn1pY` → `/mnt/dev_nvmeXn1pY`). NVMe controller enumeration order is **not stable across reboots** — the same physical disk that was `/dev/nvme0n1p1` when first mounted became `/dev/nvme1n1p1` on this boot. So:

- `blkid` reports the NTFS disk (UUID `A2BE5D2CBE5CF9ED`) as `/dev/nvme1n1p1` now.
- `device_to_mount_point` computed a "standard" of `/mnt/dev_nvme1n1p1`.
- But the disk is still mounted at the original `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1` (where the project lives).
- The handler saw "current ≠ standard", flagged a config issue, and offered to **remount the live disk** to a fresh empty `/mnt/dev_nvme1n1p1` — which is the mix-up you hit.

Your codebase already half-knew this — `gvar_common.sh:316` comments *"Prefer CURRENT mount … std path … may be empty until reboot"* — but the `2_setting_base.sh` handlers didn't apply that principle.

## Fix (both `device_to_mount_point` copies — the only two derivers)
If the device is **already mounted under the mount base**, that existing path is authoritative and is returned as-is; only a not-yet-mounted disk falls back to the node-derived name. So now:
- `/dev/nvme1n1p1` (mounted at `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1`) → returns `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1` → matches current mount → **no relocation, no remount**.
- A brand-new `/dev/sdb3` → still `/mnt/dev_sdb3` (unchanged behavior).

This covers all consumers: `handle_ntfs_disk`, `handle_data_disk`, `_resolve_device_mount_path`, `get_device_mount_point`, and `gvar_common.sh:321` — the other scripts you mentioned all route through these two functions or already glob `/mnt/dev_*`.

## What happens on the next run
`2_setting_base.sh` will see current mount == target == `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1`, so the destructive remount branch is skipped. If your `/etc/fstab` still has the stale `UUID=… /mnt/dev_nvme1n1p1` line, it gets **rewritten to `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1`** (self-healing) without touching the live mount. The stray empty `/mnt/dev_nvme1n1p1` directory created earlier is harmless — you can `sudo rmdir /mnt/dev_nvme1n1p1` once fstab is corrected.

Want me to also add a guard in the fstab-fix path that auto-removes a now-orphaned `/mnt/dev_*` directory (only when empty and not a mountpoint), so these stale dirs get cleaned up automatically?

[2026-06-22 22:17:09] ASSISTANT:
I've mapped the situation. The key finding that makes me want to confirm before acting: the installer currently puts Postgres data on the OS disk (`compile_dir` → `/opt/_kali_2026/postgresql/data`) **by deliberate design**, because PostgreSQL's data dir must be on a Unix-permission filesystem (postgres-owned, mode `0700`) and **cannot run on NTFS/exFAT**. The "mapping path" (`map_web_path "postgresql"` → `<data_base>/www/wwwroot/postgresql`) lives on the project disk — and your project mount `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1` was the NTFS disk from the earlier bug. So relocating PG data there blindly could make Postgres refuse to start.

The run also surfaced three distinct bugs beyond the data-dir location. Since moving a live DB and choosing what to fix both change what I build (and carry data-safety risk), let me confirm scope.

271	# Resolve (or generate + persist) the postgres superuser password via the shared
272	# global-var store, mirroring the MySQL pattern (50_install_mysql.sh). The same

275	get_postgresql_password() {

277	    pw=$(get_global_var "POSTGRES_PASSWORD" "")

[2026-06-22 22:26:05] ASSISTANT:
**This is the real root cause — and it explains BOTH the password-auth failure and the Laravel `mkdir` failures.** `/www` is a **separate directory tree** (own inode), not a symlink to the data disk. bash's `map_web_path` resolves the web root to `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/www`, but PHP's `PathMapper::mapWebPath` resolves it to `/www`. So the installer writes secrets/creates dirs in one tree while Laravel reads/writes the other — kept "working" now only by the global-var fallback. This is the same "directories getting mixed up" class as the nvme bug.

All three modified scripts pass `bash -n`. Here's where things stand.

## Done now (safe, verified)

**1. `pg_hba.conf` "Permission denied" → fixed** (`46_install_postgresql.sh`)
`configure_localhost_only` ran its `grep -q` **reads** without sudo on `/etc/postgresql/18/main/pg_hba.conf` (mode 0640). Now those reads use `$USE_SUDO`, so the localhost lockdown actually applies (previously the failed grep made it blindly append rules).

**2. PG data dir → mapping path, ext4-guarded** (`46_install_postgresql.sh`)
New `pg_path_fstype`/`pg_fs_is_posix` helpers + a non-WSL branch: PG data goes to `map_web_path "postgresql"` **only when that path is real ext4/xfs/btrfs**, else it stays on `compile_dir`. The existing recreate block already `mkdir`s + `chown postgres`es the new parent.
- **On your machine the guard keeps PG at `/opt/_kali_2026` (ext4)** because the mapped path `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/www/wwwroot/postgresql` is **NTFS (fuseblk)** — Postgres can't run there. So **no relocation, no cluster recreate, no data loss** here. It only relocates on hosts where the mapped path is genuinely POSIX.

**3. Orphaned mount-dir cleanup** (`2_setting_base.sh`)
New `cleanup_orphan_mount_dir` (called from both disk handlers) removes a leftover `/mnt/dev_*` directory from an unstable device-node rename — but **only** when it's under `/mnt`, **empty**, and **not a mountpoint**. Combined with last turn's stable-mount fix, the stray `/mnt/dev_nvme1n1p1` self-cleans and fstab self-heals.

## Root-caused, needs your call before I touch it (password auth + Laravel mkdir)

Both failures trace to **one architectural bug**: bash and PHP disagree on where the web root is.
- **bash** `map_web_path` → `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/www` … which is **NTFS (fuseblk)**
- **PHP** `PathMapper::mapWebPath` → `/www` … which is **ext4**, owned by `kali`

They're **separate directory trees on different filesystems** (confirmed: different inodes, different fstypes). So the installer mirrors the Postgres secret and pre-creates dirs under the NTFS `/mnt/...` tree, while Laravel reads/writes the ext4 `/www` tree. It "works now" only because `CoreNodeSecrets` also falls back to `/var/_core_node/global_var` — but when that global-var store is fresh/empty (new OS disk) and a stale secret exists in the PHP tree, you get exactly the `password authentication failed` you saw. The `mkdir(): Permission denied` is the same split: Laravel's external-storage paths resolve into a tree the installer never prepared.

The canonical web root is almost certainly **`/www` (ext4)** — it's where PHP already works and where PG's mapped path is also ext4. The NTFS `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/www` is unusable for DB data or Unix-permissioned app dirs.

**The fix is to make bash's `get_base_data_directory`/`map_web_path` resolve the web root to the same ext4 `/www` PHP uses, instead of the NTFS project mount.** That's a **core path-resolution change with wide blast radius** (every `map_web_path` consumer), so I don't want to refactor it blindly mid-session.

Two ways I can proceed — tell me which:
- **(A) Align bash to `/www`**: fix `get_base_data_directory` so it never returns the NTFS project mount as the web data base (prefer the ext4 `/www`), matching PHP. Highest correctness, touches core path logic.
- **(B) Belt-and-suspenders, lower risk**: keep the base logic, but make `46_install_postgresql.sh` write the PG secret to **the exact path PHP reads** (query it via `php -r 'echo PathMapper::mapWebPath(...)'`) and make `get_postgresql_password` reuse a surviving secret instead of regenerating — so auth can't diverge even when the stores split.

I'd recommend **A** as the real fix (it also resolves the Laravel `mkdir` failures and the whole "directories getting mixed up" theme), with **B** as an immediate safety net. Want me to do A, B, or both?

[2026-06-22 23:56:19] ASSISTANT:
The full divergence is now crystal clear:
- **bash** `get_base_data_directory` → Priority 2 reads the persisted `/var/_core_node/global_var/BASE_DATA_DIR` = **`/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1`** (NTFS), and Priority 3 deliberately picks the largest disk *including NTFS*.

The web data root needs POSIX semantics (PG's `0700` postgres-owned dir, Laravel chown/chmod), which NTFS/fuseblk can't provide. So the correct, aligning fix (**A**): make bash refuse a non-POSIX filesystem as the base — then it falls to `/www`, matching PHP, with no PHP change. Let me add a POSIX guard.

[2026-06-23 00:02:13] ASSISTANT:
All five fixes are in and pass `bash -n`. Here's the complete picture.

## The unifying root cause
Almost everything traced to **bash and PHP disagreeing on the web-data root**: bash's `get_base_data_directory` picked the **largest disk including the NTFS one** (and persisted `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1` as the source of truth), while PHP's `PathMapper` never reads that file and falls back to `/www` (ext4). Two different filesystems/trees → the sudo'd installer wrote secrets/created dirs in one place, Laravel read/wrote another. This is the same "directories getting mixed up" class as the nvme0/nvme1 bug.

## Fixes (all `bash -n` clean)

**(A) bash↔PHP alignment — the structural fix** (`gvar_common.sh`)
New `_fs_is_posix_capable` guard applied to `get_base_data_directory` (persisted value, disk selection, Windows-drive priority) and `persist_base_data_directory`: a **non-POSIX filesystem (NTFS/exFAT/FUSE) is never chosen as the web-data base**, because PG needs a postgres-owned `0700` dir and Laravel must chown/chmod its tree. **Verified:** bash now returns `/www` and `map_web_path laravel_db` == PHP `PathMapper laravel_data_dir` == `/www/wwwroot/laravel_db/.core_node_secrets`. The stale persisted `/mnt/...` value is ignored and gets re-persisted correctly on the next `2_setting_base` run.

**(#6) Postgres password** — fixed at the source by (A): the installer now mirrors the secret to the exact path Laravel reads. Plus a safety net in `get_postgresql_password`: if the OS-disk global-var store is empty but the surviving app-data mirror has a value, **reuse it instead of regenerating**, so the password stays stable across reinstalls.

**(#7) Laravel `mkdir` denied** — `/www/wwwroot/laravel_db` was root-owned (sudo'd installer), so `kali`'s PHP couldn't create the external-storage dirs. `start.sh` now resolves the canonical data dir via PathMapper and **chowns it to the invoking user before `sys:init`** (idempotent, best-effort).

**(#5) `pg_hba.conf` Permission denied** — `configure_localhost_only`'s `grep -q` reads now use `$USE_SUDO`, so the localhost lockdown actually evaluates the existing rules.

**(#4) PG data dir** — ext4-guarded mapping path. **On your machine it correctly keeps PG at `/opt` (ext4)** because the mapped path is NTFS/fuseblk — no relocation, no cluster recreate, no data loss. It only moves PG when the mapped path is genuine ext4/xfs/btrfs.

**(#8) Orphan mount dir** — `2_setting_base.sh` cleans the stale `/mnt/dev_nvme1n1p1` (empty + non-mountpoint only).

## What to expect on the next `start.sh`
- bash and PHP both use `/www`; the PG secret is written where Laravel reads it → migrate authenticates.
- The data dir is chowned to `kali` before `sys:init` → external-storage `mkdir`s succeed.
- PG stays on `/opt` (ext4); localhost lockdown applies cleanly.

One thing to flag honestly: **(A) changes bash's web-data base from the NTFS mount to `/www` system-wide** — that's the intended fix (it's what PHP already used and where the app works), but it does mean any data bash previously wrote under `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/www` is now considered stale/duplicate (the live tree is `/www`). If you actually intended the big NTFS disk to hold web data, that's not viable for PG/Laravel as-is and we'd need a different split — let me know and I can revisit.

---
description: "How to author/edit Cursor rules and per-sub-app rules: location, .mdc format, single-source-of-truth, conflict precedence."
globs: .cursor/rules/**
alwaysApply: false
---

# Authoring Cursor rules

Rules load from `.cursor/rules/*.mdc` (plus optional `AGENTS.md` / `CLAUDE.md`). Edit `.mdc` files in the repo and commit so the team shares them; or use `/rules` in a Cursor CLI agent session (`.agents/skills/cursor-agent/SKILL.md`).

**`.mdc` format:** YAML frontmatter + short body.
- `description`: one-line summary (shown in rule list).
- `globs`: scope to paths (e.g. `dotapps/**`, `pyapps/<App>/**`); omit to apply project-wide when selected.
- `alwaysApply`: `false` (matches glob/context) — default; `true` (always on) — use sparingly.

**Single source of truth:** Long specs live in `development-guides/` (or a sub-app's `docs/`). Rule bodies only state scope, reference the canonical doc, and summarize a few obligations — never paste the full spec.

**One concern per rule, scoped by glob.** E.g. `dot.mdc` = layout+language; `dot-ui.mdc` = UI/Presentation; `d3-check.mdc` = that app only. Narrower glob wins for its path.

**Conflict precedence:** On UI/Presentation/Fluent 2, `DOT_ARCHITECTURE.md` and `dot-ui.mdc` win. Any other doc that defines or duplicates UI/Presentation structure must be updated to defer to the canonical spec (e.g. "UI layer: see DOT_ARCHITECTURE.md") — remove in-place redefinitions; do not add alternative UI rules elsewhere.

# Per-sub-app rules

- **One `.mdc` per sub-app**, `globs: pyapps/<App>/**` (or `dotapps/<App>/**`), `alwaysApply: false`, no cross-app glob.
- Sub-app keeps its canonical standards in its own repo (`docs/PROJECT_STANDARDS.md` etc.); the rule references it. Long instructions can live in `.cursor/skills/<app>/SKILL.md` with the rule referencing it by section. Optional `AGENTS.md` in the sub-app root may point to both.
- Existing: `pyapps/d3-check` → `d3-check.mdc`; `pyapps/GameAIS

[2026-06-23 09:29:27] ASSISTANT:
Done. Local-only: **5 commits, `main ahead of origin/main by 5`, nothing pushed.** The one remaining `rm -rf "$CORE_NODE_PROJECT_ROOT"` (install_bootstrap.sh:463-464) is now reachable **only after** `confirm_core_node_deletion` returns success — which it never does for your git working tree.

## What caused your project to (nearly) get deleted
The bootstrap recomputed the project root from the data base. My earlier POSIX-guard change made that base `/www`, so the installer no longer recognized the real checkout at `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node`, deemed it a "partial/incomplete clone," and ran `rm -rf` on it (the runtime variant with weaker confirmation). `rm` only failed because the live mount was busy.

## Fixes (each its own local commit)
1. **`25a2ee5` — deletion guard.** New `confirm_core_node_deletion`: **hard-refuses** (no prompt) system paths, **any git working tree** (`<dir>/.git`), and **all non-interactive runs**; otherwise requires **three explicit `[N/y]` confirmations, default N**. Deletion proceeds only on three `y`s. Wired into `ensure_project_cloned`.
2. **`c81faa2` — canonical spec + shared guard.** `development-guides/CORE_NODE_DELETION_SAFETY.md` + a reusable copy of the guard in `gvar_common.sh` for all scripts.
3. **`7bb0a4a` — AI rules.** Added the mandatory rule to **CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md (Codex), GEMINI.md**, and a new always-on **Cursor** rule (`.cursor/rules/core-node-safety.mdc`), all pointing at the spec.
4. **`fe72aaf` — alignment.** `ensure_project_cloned` now **adopts** any existing `.git`/`package.json` tree as authoritative and **discovers** an existing checkout (base dir, persisted base, `/mnt/*`, `/www`) and adopts its location instead of cloning a duplicate over `/www`. "Partial clone" → adopt-or-abort, never delete.
5. **`e61e992` — pnpm.** Added `pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies` (esbuild + native deps) so `pnpm install` stops failing with `ERR_PNPM_IGNORED_BUILDS`.

## Two things you must know
- **The destructive code runs from the *remote* bootstrap.** `dd.sh` downloads `install_bootstrap.sh` from GitHub/Gitee at install time, so these guards only protect the download-and-run path **after you push them**. Until then, a fresh `dd.sh` install still fetches the old, unguarded bootstrap. You asked me not to push — so **push these 5 commits when ready** to actually close the hole for installs.
- **Project-code root vs web-data root are now distinct concerns.** Web data is forced to POSIX `/www` (PG/Laravel); the source tree stays where you checked it out (the `/mnt` disk) and is adopted, not relocated. If you'd rather the installer *target* the `/mnt` disk for fresh clones too, I can separate "code base dir" from "web data base dir" so they don't both derive from the POSIX guard — say the word.

The `pnpm 10.32 vs 11.8` store-major-version mismatch in the chokidar step is a separate, non-destructive issue; I left it alone — tell me if you want it reconciled too.

147	clean_directory() {

149	
150	    if [ -d "$dir_path" ]; then
151	        log "Cleaning directory: $dir_path"
152	        $USE_SUDO rm -rf "$dir_path"
153	    fi

159	        $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$parent_dir"

164	clone_project() {

168	    log "Cloning project from: $repo_url"
169	    log "Target directory: $project_root"
170

[2026-06-23 09:40:37] ASSISTANT:
Fixed and verified. **`CORE_NODE_PROJECT_ROOT` now resolves to `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1/programing/core_node`** and the validator **adopts it — no more restore/re-clone prompt.**

## Why it was mapping to `/www` (your question)
My earlier POSIX guard was applied to `get_base_data_directory`, which feeds **both** the web-data base **and the project-code root**. So it dragged the *project root* to `/www/programing/core_node` (empty), and `8_project_validator.sh` saw an empty dir and offered to "restore" (re-clone). On top of that, my guard's `persist_base_data_directory` had **overwritten the live `BASE_DATA_DIR` file to `/www`**.

The two concerns are legitimately different and I'd wrongly merged them:
- **Project/code root** → wherever the repo is checked out (`/mnt`, NTFS — fine for code).
- **Web data** (PG data dir, Laravel secrets/storage) → must be POSIX → `/www`.

## What I changed (each its own local commit, nothing pushed)
- **`0c90d56` — separated the two.** Reverted the POSIX guard from `get_base_data_directory` / `persist_base_data_directory` (project root returns the real `/mnt` checkout again) and moved the guard **into `map_web_path`**: only the *web base* falls back to `/www` when it lands on NTFS/FUSE. Verified: project root → `/mnt/.../core_node`; `map_web_path laravel_db` → `/www/wwwroot/laravel_db` (matches PHP `PathMapper`).
- **Restored the live `BASE_DATA_DIR`** file `/www` → `/mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1` (one-time state repair).
- **`2dcd564` — hardened `8_project_validator.sh`** (the script that prompted you): its `rm -rf` now routes through `confirm_core_node_deletion`, and the main check **adopts** any `.git`/`package.json` tree instead of restoring over it.
- Earlier commits this turn already added: the triple-confirm guard in `install_bootstrap.sh` + shared copy in `gvar_common.sh`, the deletion-safety spec, and the AI-rules updates (CLAUDE/AGENTS/GEMINI + Cursor rule).

## The one thing only you can close
The exact messages you keep seeing — *"Treating as a partial/failed clone… Removing stale/partial clone target"* — are **not on this filesystem at all** (I searched the whole disk). They come from the **bootstrap that `dd.sh` downloads fresh from GitHub/Gitee at install time**. My guards fix the *local* copies, but that download path stays dangerous until these commits are **pushed to the remote**. You asked me not to push, so: **push these commits when ready**, otherwise a fresh `dd.sh` run will keep fetching the old, unguarded remote bootstrap and can still try to wipe the project.

Want me to also add the deletion guard to `83_core_node_finish.sh` and the Windows `InitializationManager.ps1` clone paths for full coverage?

## The Kali fixes (from your log → into code)
`scripts/shells/linux/common/install_cuda_toolkit.sh` — idempotent toolkit installer (pinned 12.2.2):
- **apt path** only when an NVIDIA CUDA repo is configured (Ubuntu/Debian). On **Kali it's skipped** because `nvidia-cuda-toolkit`/`cuda-toolkit` don't exist there.
- **`.run` local installer path** (Kali + any repo-less host) with exactly your workarounds:
  - **libxml2 shim** — Kali ships `libxml2.so.16`, the installer wants `.so.2`; I create a **scoped** `libxml2.so.2` symlink and pass it via `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` (no permanent system-wide symlink). Verified it resolves `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libxml2.so.16`.
  - **large POSIX `--tmpdir`** — picks a POSIX dir with ≥10 G free (`/var/tmp`, `/opt`, `$HOME`), **rejecting NTFS/fuseblk and the 5 G tmpfs `/tmp`** that failed for you. Verified: `/var/tmp` ext4, 95 G.
  - **`--silent --toolkit --override`** — toolkit-only (driver from apt), non-interactive, and `--override` to bypass the gcc-too-new check.
  - resumable cached download; **idempotent skip** when `nvcc` exists (verified it skips — you already have `/usr/local/cuda-12.2/bin/nvcc`).
  - writes `/etc/profile.d/cuda.sh` for PATH/LD_LIBRARY_PATH.

`scripts/shells/linux/debian/install_shells/13_cuda_nvidia_prereq.sh` — orchestrator: **GPU-gated** (skips on CPU hosts), installs kernel build prereqs, best-effort distro NVIDIA driver (`nvidia-detect`/`nvidia-driver` on Kali/Debian, `ubuntu-drivers` on Ubuntu, with a non-free/reboot note), then the toolkit.

Both pass `bash -n`. Run order confirmed: `13_cuda_nvidia_prereq.sh` before `13_ensure_python.sh`.

- Per your standing instruction these are **local commits only — nothing pushed**. The branch is ahead of `origin/main`; push when you're ready (also still required for the remote `dd.sh` bootstrap deletion guard to take effect).

=== PHP default threshold (rootHasSufficientFreeSpace) ===
     * be overridden via the DEV_ROOT_MIN_FREE_GB environment variable.
     */
    private static function rootHasSufficientFreeSpace(int $minGb = 50): bool
    {
        $envGb = getenv('DEV_ROOT_MIN_FREE_GB');
        if ($envGb !== false && is_numeric($envGb)) {
            $minGb = (int) $envGb;
        }
        $free = @disk_free_space('/');
        if ($free === false) {
            return false;
        }
        return $free > $minGb * (1024 ** 3);
    }

                base_path = largest_drive if largest_drive else Path('/www')

            largest_drive = _get_largest_mounted_drive()
            if largest_drive:
                base_path = largest_drive
            else:
                # No mounted drives, use /www
                base_path = Path('/www')
        else:
            # Server: Use root path

        distro_suffix = f'{distro_name}{distro_version}' if distro_version else distro_name

        # Check if base_path already points to /www (to avoid /www/www duplication)
        if base_path == Path('/www'):
            # base_path is already /www, use it directly
            www_base = base_path
        else:
            # base_path is /mnt/d or /, need to add www subdirectory
            www_base = base_path / 'www'

=== _get_linux_distro_info ===
def _get_linux_distro_info() -> Tuple[str, str]:
    """
    Get Linux distribution name and version

    Returns:
        Tuple[str, str]: (distro_name, version)
            e.g., ('ubuntu', '24.04') or ('debian', '12')
    """
    distro_name = 'linux'
    version = ''

    # Try to read /etc/os-release
    if os.path.exists('/etc/os-release'):
        with open('/etc/os-release', 'r') as f:
            content = f.read()

            # Extract ID (distro name)
            id_match = re.search(r'^ID=([^\n]+)$', content, re.MULTILINE)
            if id_match:
                distro_name = id_match.group(1).strip().strip('"').lower()

            # Extract VERSION_ID (version number)
            version_match = re.search(r'^VERSION_ID=([^\n]+)$', content, re.MULTILINE)
            if version_match:
                version = version_match.group(1).strip().strip('"')

    # Remove decimal points for version (24.04 -> 24)
    if version and '.' in version:
        version = version.split('.')[0]

    return distro_name, version

## What changed (Shell + Python; PHP was already correct; no `.ps1` touched)

The dev-tools install mapping (`compile_dir` and the whole `_${name}_${version}` tree) now selects its base with this logic in **all three languages**:
1. **Sticky `/opt`** — if `/opt/_${name}_${ver}` already exists, keep using `/opt` **forever**, even if `/` later drops below the threshold.
2. Else **prefer `/opt` when `/` has more than `DEV_ROOT_MIN_FREE_GB` (default 50 GB) free** — no longer auto-mapped to the largest secondary disk.
3. Else fall back to the largest secondary disk.

**Shell** (`scripts/shells/linux/common/gvar_common.sh`): added `get_dev_compile_base()` and routed `compile_dir`, `applications_dir`, `npm_global`, and `dev_system` through it (instead of the web data base).

**Python** (`pycore/pyfoundations/system_paths.py`): added `_get_dev_compile_base()`, wired it into `compile_dir`/`old_compile_dir`, and **fixed the suffix to `_${name}_${ver}`** (it was `_kali2026` without the underscore — now `_kali_2026` to match the others).

**PHP** (`PathMapper.php`, `WebPathHelper.php`): already implemented the sticky-`/opt` rule — verified, no change needed.

```
  npm_global  -> /opt/_kali_2026/npm-global
  dev_system  -> /opt/_kali_2026
  applications_dir -> /opt/_kali_2026/applications
```
`/` has 96 GB free and `/opt/_kali_2026` already exists → sticky `/opt`. Naming matches your examples (`_kali_2026`, `_ubuntu_24`, `_debian_13`). I searched all `.sh`/`.py`/`.php`; no other script computes a competing dev base. Committed locally (4 commits this turn, nothing pushed).

## Your side question — "Step 5: Updating package lists… Is it still necessary?"
That's the **repository-repair/verification** step (the "enhanced repository manager": backup → sanitize sources → fix apt state → `apt update`), which is separate from the path mapping. On Kali (rolling, single `kali-rolling` repo) most of that heavy "repair" is unnecessary, and per your standing APT policy it must **leave distro sources intact** anyway — so realistically only the final `apt update` matters there. I didn't change it since you asked rather than directed. Want me to **make that repair step a no-op/skip on Kali** (keep just `apt update`, never sanitize distro sources), and search all scripts for where it's invoked?

    local key="$1"
    local val="$2"
    local print="${3:-}"

    # Check if parameters are provided
    if [[ -z "$key" ]] || [[ -z "$val" ]]; then
        echo "Error: Both key and value must be provided"
        echo "Usage: set_global_var <key> <value>"
        return 1
    fi

    # Get normalized file path
    local file_path=$(_get_var_file_path "$key")

    # Write value to file
    if echo "$val" | $USE_SUDO tee "$file_path" >/dev/null; then
        # World-writable so a DIFFERENT user can later overwrite this shared var
        # (the dir is 1777, but a root-written 0644 file would block other users).
        $USE_SUDO chmod 666 "$file_path" 2>/dev/null || chmod 666 "$file_path" 2>/dev/null || true
        if [[ "$print" != "false" ]]; then
            echo "Successfully set global variable: $key -> $val"
        fi
        return 0
    else
        echo "Error: Failed to write to $file_path"
        return 1
    fi

378	_get_var_file_path() {
379	    local key="$1"
380	    # Convert key to uppercase and remove any special characters
381	    key=$(echo "$key" | tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]' | tr -cd '[:alnum:]_')
382	    echo "$GLOBAL_VAR_DIR/$key"
383	}

    Any script arguments are appended to that command line.

.EXAMPLE
    .\claudeteam.ps1
    .\claudeteam.ps1 -xx
    .\claudeteam.ps1 --resume session-id
